4.1
. Now

\textcircled{\small{b}}\leq(L+\lambda R)\left[\sqrt{\frac{2\alpha}{\lambda}}+\mathbb{E}\|z_{\lambda}\|_{2}\right]+\frac{\lambda R^{2}}{2},

by decomposing and bounding as done in the proof of
theorem

C.1
. Also, \small{c}⃝
\leq 0
as usual. Then combining the above with our choices of
\lambda,\alpha,T
and using
lemma

B.3
completes the proof.

D.3.3
Proof of of
proposition

D.2

To begin, we follow along the same lines as in the proof of
proposition

D.1
:

\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathcal{D}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}}F(w_{\mathcal{A},\lambda}(X),\mathcal{D})-F(w^{*}(\mathcal{D}),\mathcal{D})=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathcal{D}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}}[F(w_{\mathcal{A}}(X),\mathcal{D})-\widehat{F}(w_{\mathcal{A}}(X),X)]}_{\textcircled{\small{a}}}+\\
\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathcal{D}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}}[\widehat{F}(w_{\mathcal{A}}(X),X)-\widehat{F}(\widehat{w}(X),X)]}_{\textcircled{\small{b}}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathcal{D}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}}\widehat{F}(\widehat{w}(X),X)-F(w^{*}(\mathcal{D}),\mathcal{D})}_{\textcircled{\small{c}}}.\\

Then
\textcircled{\small{a}}\leq\frac{2(L+\lambda R)^{2}}{\lambda n}
as in the proof of
proposition

D.1
. Next, decompose \small{b}⃝ into three parts as in the proof of
proposition

2.7
:

\displaystyle\textcircled{\small{b}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{F}(w_{\mathcal{A}}(X),X)-\widehat{F_{\lambda}}(w_{\mathcal{A}}(X),X)]}_{\textcircled{\small{1}}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{F_{\lambda}}(w_{\mathcal{A}}(X),X)-\widehat{F_{\lambda}}(\widehat{w_{\lambda}}(X),X)]}_{\textcircled{\small{2}}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}[\widehat{F_{\lambda}}(\widehat{w_{\lambda}})(X),X)-\widehat{F}(\widehat{w}(X),X)]}_{\textcircled{\small{3}}}.

Then
\textcircled{1}\leq 0
and
\textcircled{\small{3}}\leq\frac{\lambda R^{2}}{2},
as in the proof of
proposition

D.1
, but to bound \small{2}⃝, we now use the descent lemma to obtain

\textcircled{\small{2}}\leq\frac{(\beta+\lambda)}{2}\mathbb{E}\|z_{\lambda}\|_{2}^{2}.

By the definition of
z_{\lambda}
(
section

2.4
),
lemma

B.3
, and
proposition

2.2
, we have
\mathbb{E}\|z_{\lambda}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 8\frac{(L+\lambda R)^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\left(\frac{d}{\varepsilon n}\right)^{2}
if
\delta=0;
if
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2}),
then
\mathbb{E}\|z_{\lambda}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{4(L+\lambda R)^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}\left(c_{\delta}+\sqrt{c_{\delta}^{2}+\varepsilon}\right)}{\varepsilon n}\right)^{2}.

The third term
\textcircled{\small{3}}\leq\frac{\lambda R^{2}}{2}.
Lastly,
\textcircled{\small{c}}\leq 0
by the same argument used in
proposition

4.1
. Hence if
~\delta=0,
then

\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathcal{D}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}}F(w_{\mathcal{A},\lambda}(X),\mathcal{D})-F(w^{*}(\mathcal{D}),\mathcal{D})\leq\frac{2(L+\lambda R)^{2}}{\lambda n}+4\frac{(\beta+\lambda)(L+\lambda R)^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\left(\frac{d}{\varepsilon n}\right)^{2}+\frac{\lambda R^{2}}{2}.

If
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2}),
then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathcal{D}^{n},\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}}F(w_{\mathcal{A},\lambda}^{\delta}(X),\mathcal{D})-F(w^{*}(\mathcal{D}),\mathcal{D})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(L+\lambda R)^{2}}{\lambda n}

\displaystyle+2\frac{(\beta+\lambda)(L+\lambda R)^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}\left(c_{\delta}+\sqrt{c_{\delta}^{2}+\varepsilon}\right)}{\varepsilon n}\right)^{2}+\frac{\lambda R^{2}}{2}.

Set
\lambda=\left(\frac{\beta L^{2}}{R^{2}}\right)^{1/3}\left(\left(\frac{d}{\varepsilon n}\right)^{2/3}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)
and use the assumptions stated in the Proposition to get
\lambda\leq 5\beta.
Then bound
\frac{2(L+\lambda R)^{2}}{\lambda n}\leq 2\beta^{1/3}L^{2/3}R^{4/3}\left(\left(\frac{d}{\varepsilon n}\right)^{2/3}+\frac{5}{\sqrt{n}}\right);

4\frac{(\beta+\lambda)(L+\lambda R)^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\left(\frac{d}{\varepsilon n}\right)^{2}\leq 80\beta^{1/3}L^{2/3}R^{4/3}\left(\frac{d}{\varepsilon n}\right)^{2/3};
and
\frac{\lambda R^{2}}{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\beta^{1/3}L^{2/3}R^{4/3}\left(\left(\frac{d}{\varepsilon n}\right)^{2/3}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)
to botain the first part of the Proposition.

For
\delta\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right),
instead choose
\lambda=\left(\frac{\beta L^{2}}{R^{2}}\right)^{1/3}\left(\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}\left(c_{\delta}+\sqrt{c_{\delta}^{2}+\varepsilon}\right)}{\varepsilon n}\right)^{2/3}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)
and proceed similarly to get the second bound.

D.3.4
Proof of
corollary

D.2

The proof follows almost exactly as the proof of
corollary

D.1
, but uses the descent lemma to bound \small{b}⃝ with a
\frac{(\beta+\lambda)}{2}\mathbb{E}\|z_{\lambda}\|^{2}
term (instead of
(L+\lambda R)\mathbb{E}\|z_{\lambda}\|_{2}
) and uses

corollary

C.2
instead of
corollary

C.1
(along with the alternative choices of
\alpha
and
\lambda
) to obtain the stated bounds in faster runtime.

Appendix E
Proofs of Results in
section

5

E.1
Proofs of Results in
section

5.1

E.1.1
Proof of
lemma

5.2

First note that
\Delta_{F_{\tau}}\leq\frac{2L}{\mu}
follows immediately from
proposition

2.2
.
To prove
\Delta_{F_{\tau}}\leq\frac{2LC_{\tau}}{\mu n},
we follow the same approach used in proving
proposition

2.2
. Let
X,X^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}
such that
|X\Delta X^{\prime}|\leq 2
and assume WLOG that
x_{n}\neq x_{n}^{\prime}.
We apply
lemma

B.2
with
g_{\tau}(w):=F_{\tau}(w,X)-F_{\tau}(w,X^{\prime})

and
G_{\tau}(w):=F_{\tau}(w,X^{\prime}).
Denote
v_{i}(\tau,w)=\frac{e^{\tau f(w,x_{i})}}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}e^{\tau f(w,x_{j})}}
and
v_{i}^{\prime}(\tau,w)=\frac{e^{\tau f(w,x_{i}^{\prime})}}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}e^{\tau f(w,x_{j}^{\prime})}}.
Then observe that

\displaystyle\nabla g_{\tau}(w)

\displaystyle=\nabla F_{\tau}(w,X)-\nabla F_{\tau}(w,X^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}(\tau,w)\nabla f(w,x_{i})-v_{i}^{\prime}(\tau,w)\nabla f(w,x_{i}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=v_{n}(\tau,w)\nabla f(w,x_{n})-v_{n}^{\prime}(\tau,w)\nabla f(w,x_{n}^{\prime}).

Now convexity and
L
-Lipschitzness of
f
imply

\|\nabla g_{\tau}(w)\|\leq 2L\max\{v_{n}(\tau,w),v_{n}^{\prime}(\tau,w)\}\leq 2LC_{\tau}.

This proof is completed by noticing that
G_{\tau}
is
\mu
-strongly convex and appealing to
lemma

B.2
.

E.1.2
Proof of
lemma

5.3

First, we have
\nabla F_{\tau}(w,X)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}(w,\tau)\nabla f_{i}(w),
where
v_{i}(w,\tau):=\frac{e^{\tau f(w,x_{i})}}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}e^{\tau f(w,x_{j})}}
and we denote
f_{i}(w):=f(w,x_{i}),
by
eq.

9
. Now for any
w_{1},w_{2}\in B(0,R)
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla F_{\tau}(w_{1},X)-\nabla F_{\tau}(w_{2},X)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(v_{i}(w_{1},\tau)\nabla f_{i}(w_{1})-v_{i}(w_{2},\tau)f_{i}(w_{2})\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(v_{i}(w_{1},\tau)\beta\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{2}+L_{v_{i}}\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{2}L\right)

\displaystyle\leq\beta\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{2}+L\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(L_{v_{i}}\right),

where
L_{v_{i}}
denotes the Lipschitz constant of
v_{i}(w,\tau)
as a function of
w.

Next, we compute
L_{v_{i}}
by bounding
\|\nabla v_{i}(w,\tau)\|
:

\displaystyle\nabla v_{i}(w,\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau e^{\tau f_{i}(w)}\nabla f_{i}(w)\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}e^{\tau f_{j}(w)}\right)-\tau\sum_{j=1}^{n}e^{\tau f_{j}(w)}\nabla f_{j}(w)e^{\tau f_{i}(w)}}{\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}e^{\tau f_{j}(w)}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau\sum_{j=1}^{n}e^{\tau f_{j}(w)}e^{\tau f_{i}(w)}\left(\nabla f_{i}(w)-\nabla f_{j}(w)\right)}{\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}e^{\tau f_{j}(w)}\right)^{2}}.

Taking the norm of both sides and using
L
-Lipschitzness of
f(\cdot,x_{i})
implies

L_{v_{i}}\leq 2L\tau\frac{e^{\tau f_{i}(w)}}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}e^{\tau f_{j}(w)}},

and hence

\sum_{i=1}^{n}L_{v_{i}}\leq 2L\tau.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\nabla F_{\tau}(w_{1},X)-\nabla F_{\tau}(w_{2},X)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\beta\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{2}+L\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}L_{v_{i}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\beta+2L^{2}\tau\right)\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|_{2},

which proves
lemma

5.3
.

E.2
Proofs of Results in
section

5.2

E.2.1
Proof of
lemma

5.4

First of all,
g
is
L
-Lipschitz on
B(0,R)
if and only if
\|q\|_{2}\leq L
for all subgradients
q\in\partial g(w)
for all
w\in B(0,R).
Moreover, for any
w\in B(0,R),

\partial g(w)=conv\left(\bigcup\{u\in\partial_{w}h(w,\widehat{v})\colon\widehat{v}\in\argmax_{v}h(w,v)\}\right),

where
conv
denotes the convex hull of a set. Then since
\|u\|\leq L
for all
u\in\partial_{w}h(w,\widehat{v})
(as
h(\cdot,\widehat{v})
is
L
-Lipschitz by assumption), we have
\|q\|_{2}\leq L
for all
q\in\partial g(w),
which establishes Lipschitzness of
g.

Next, consider the convex (in
w
for all
v\in S
) function
\widetilde{h}(w,v):=h(w,v)-\frac{\mu}{2}\|w\|_{2}^{2}.
Then since the maximum of a (possibly uncountable) family of convex functions is convex, we have that
\max_{v\in S}\widetilde{h}(w,v)=g(w)-\frac{\mu}{2}\|w\|_{2}^{2}
is convex, which means that
g
is
\mu
-strongly convex.

Appendix F
L_{2}
Sensitivity Lower Bound for
\mathcal{F}_{\mu,L,R}

In this section, we establish that the sensitivity analysis, which is essential to most of our results, is tight. We do so by exhibiting a data universe
\mathcal{X}:=B(0,1)
and a function
F\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu,L,R}^{ERM}
with
\Delta_{F}\geq\frac{L}{\mu n}.
Our hard instance is
f(w,x):=\frac{\mu}{4}\|w\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{L}{4}x^{T}w,
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times B(0,1).
Then

\nabla f(w,x)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mu w+\frac{L}{2}x\right)\implies\|\nabla f(w,x)\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\mu R+\frac{L}{2}\right)\leq L,

for any
L\geq\mu R
. So,
f(\cdot,x)
is
L
-Lipschitz for any
L\geq\mu R
(for all
x\in\mathcal{X}
). Furthermore
\nabla^{2}f(w,x)=\mu\mathbf{I}.
Define
F(w,X)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(w,x_{i}).
Observe that for any
X=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in B(0,1),
we have

\nabla F(w,X)=\frac{\mu}{2}w+\frac{L}{4n}\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}\implies w^{*}(X)=\frac{-L}{2\mu n}\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}.

Hence
\|w^{*}(X)\|_{2}\leq R
for any
R\geq\frac{L}{\mu}
(for all
X\in\mathcal{X}
), so
F\in\mathcal{F}_{\mu,L,R}^{ERM}.
Now consider the data sets
X=\{0,...,0,e_{1}\}
and
X^{\prime}=\{0,...,0,-e_{1}\},
where
e_{1}=(1,0,...,0)^{T}\in\mathcal{X}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}.
We will show that
\|w^{*}(X)-w^{*}(X^{\prime})\|\geq\frac{L}{\mu n},
which implies our lower bound
\Delta_{F}\geq\frac{L}{\mu n}.

Consider

\nabla F(w,X)=\frac{\mu}{2}w+\frac{L}{4}e_{1}=0\implies w^{*}(X)=-\frac{L}{2\mu n}e_{1}

and likewise,
w^{*}(X^{\prime})=\frac{L}{2\mu n}e_{1}.
Thus,
\|w^{*}(X)-w^{*}(X^{\prime})\|_{2}=\frac{L}{\mu n}.
